# ContrataCR — Design & UX Guide

> **READ THIS FIRST.** Every task that touches UI, layout, copy, or visual design **must read this file before writing any code** and conform to it. If a change would contradict this guide, follow the guide (or, if the guide is genuinely wrong for the case, note it and update this file in the same change). The goal is a single, cohesive, dead-simple product — not many locally-clever screens.

ContrataCR is a Costa Rican services marketplace (clients find and book professionals). Our users value apps that are **very easy and intuitive**. The reference for structure and UX patterns is a clean service-directory experience in the spirit of **Hulihealth** (well-organized search with a results list + map, compact information-dense cards, clear availability). We replicate the **structure and patterns**, never their code, assets, or brand — everything uses ContrataCR's own brand, colors, content, and features.

> **The unified design system (authoritative).** Two values rank above all: **(1) EASE OF USE** — every screen must be effortless for any Costa Rican, tech-savvy or not; **(2) CONSISTENCY** — the same patterns everywhere, so it always feels like ONE app. **The home page (`/`) is the quality bar** — the screen we're happiest with; new/edited screens should feel like they belong next to it (brand: navy `#1a2744`/`#162543`, blue `#009FD9`/`#008ce0`, Poppins-style bold wordmark). The canonical kit every screen composes from: **tokens in §2** (color, type scale, spacing, radius) + the **shared primitives** `Button` · `Input`/`Select` · `Badge` · `Card` · `Avatar` (already conform — reuse, don't re-derive), the **one app-wide `LandingNavbar` + `LandingFooter`** (§"Header, footer"), and the **shared patterns** in §3 (cards, lists, modals, empty/feedback states). **Feedback is inline, not toasts** (there is no toast library by design): inline error/success blocks + the always-visible autosave status line (§"Save feedback"). When a screen looks "off", it's almost always a §2.Radius violation (nested bordered boxes) or a bespoke control that should have been a primitive — fix it toward this standard.

---

## 1. Core principle — simplicity first

**Function means nothing if it's cluttered or confusing.** Every screen must be immediately understandable by a non-technical Costa Rican user on a phone.

Apply, in order:
1. **One primary action per screen.** Make the most common next step obvious (one filled primary button). Everything else is secondary/quiet.
2. **Show only what's needed now.** Hide advanced or rare options behind collapsible sections, "+N" overflows, popovers, or drawers. Never dump every field/option on screen at once.
3. **No redundancy.** One piece of information appears in exactly one place. One validation error shows once. Never two messages saying the same thing.
4. **Group related things; separate unrelated things.** Use clear visual grouping (cards/sections), consistent spacing, and a clear hierarchy (size, weight, color) so the eye lands on the right thing first.
5. **Compact but legible.** Dense like a good directory — no wasted vertical space — but never cramped or tiny to the point of being hard to read (see §5).
6. **Consistency beats cleverness.** Reuse the tokens, components, and patterns below. A familiar pattern used everywhere is easier than a new bespoke one per screen.

When in doubt, remove. If a block doesn't help the user complete their task, cut it.

**Microcopy is brief.** Explanatory body text (banners, cards, status panels) should be 1-2 short sentences that say what to do + the one benefit, never a paragraph. Don't restate what an adjacent control/button already conveys (e.g. if a WhatsApp button is right there, don't also write "you can message us on WhatsApp"). The pro Verificacion panel uses one clean status card at the top (icon + title + short body; `Badge variant="verified"` only when verified), then action cards below. **"Que es la verificacion de identidad?"** / **"What is identity verification?"** stays as a simple brand-blue text link (`#009FD9`) inside the ID action card, with no border, background, icon, or arrow.

---

## 1.5 Usability hard rules (R1–R10) — non-negotiable

These are **enforceable acceptance criteria**, not suggestions. Every screen must pass all ten; a violation is a bug to fix, not a style preference. Audit any screen you touch against this list.

- **R1 — No nested bordered containers.** Max ONE level of bordered card per content block. A bordered card inside a bordered card (inside a bordered card) is forbidden. For inner structure use spacing, dividers (`border-t border-[#f3f4f6]`), or typography — not another box. (Tinted callouts like the amber notice count as a border level.)
- **R2 — No truncation/overflow at 360px.** Names, labels, placeholders, buttons, and chips must show fully at ~360px: wrap, shrink, or reflow. Never `…`/cut-off on critical info (a person's name, a price, a primary button label). `truncate` is allowed only on genuinely secondary, repeated metadata — never on the one thing the user needs.
- **R3 — One obvious primary action.** Every screen answers "what do I do here" with exactly ONE visually dominant primary (filled brand) button, reachable in ≤2 clicks from its section. Everything else is secondary/quiet.
- **R4 — No dead ends.** Every screen/state has an obvious way back/forward (back to panel, continue, cancel). Every empty state = a message + a next action (button/link), never a blank area.
- **R5 — No unexplained controls.** Every toggle/checkbox/icon-button has a visible label or immediate explanation. No mystery icons (a lone check/x/lock without text or an `aria-label` + tooltip).
- **R6 — Forms.** Required fields marked (`*`); correct mobile keyboards (`inputMode`/`type`/`autoComplete`); errors shown inline next to the field in friendly Costa Rican Spanish (no voseo); submit button always visible/reachable on mobile (never off-screen or behind overflow).
- **R7 — Consistency.** The same element looks and behaves the same everywhere — one button set, one chip style, one card pattern, one filter-tab pattern. Same-purpose sections across the two dashboards share the same layout pattern.
- **R8 — Feedback.** Every action gives immediate feedback (saving/saved, sent, error). No silent saves, no ambiguity about success. Use inline blocks + the autosave status line — never leave the user guessing.
- **R9 — Dismissal & selection.** Click-outside closes dropdowns/popovers/autocompletes; ESC closes modals; tapping a suggestion fills the field (it never auto-executes a search or submit).
- **R10 — English parity.** Anything you touch must be fully bilingual: no Spanish leaking into EN, no layout breaking with longer English strings. (Admin panel is the sole Spanish-only exception.)

**Off-limits to UI/usability passes:** the cédula/padrón verification flow (`/api/cedula/[id]`, `identity-verifier.ts`, `run-verification.ts`, the `IdentityField` fetch/branching), auth, booking, and payment logic. If a usability fix would require touching those, **flag it — do not change it.**

> **Request vocabulary — direct vs open, per side (sprint 427).** A client can reach a pro two ways, and each side names them so they never blur. **CLIENT side:** **"Mis solicitudes"** = DIRECT one-to-one requests (found a pro, requested the service directly); **"Mis publicaciones"** (short "Publicaciones"; EN "My posts"/"Posts") = OPEN postings (described a need openly → receives propuestas). Only ONE client section says "solicitudes" — the open one deliberately uses "publicación" (not "solicitudes publicadas") so the two are instantly distinct. **PRO side mirror:** **"Solicitudes recibidas"** = the direct solicitudes clients sent them; **"Oportunidades"** = open postings to propose on. So a client's *solicitud* → the pro's *Solicitudes recibidas*; a client's *publicación* → the pro's *Oportunidades*. Keep these four labels distinct; never label a client section "Solicitudes publicadas" again (it blurred with "Mis solicitudes"). Internal ids stay `sent_bookings`/`sent_projects` (use) and `bookings`/`proposals` (offer).



> **Oportunidades summary/expand contract (sprint 533).** Oportunidades cards use the SAME accordion model on desktop and mobile: cards are summarized by default, and expanding happens inline below that same card (no desktop master-detail side panel). The closed summary owns **title, Nueva badge, budget, zone, timeline, posted time, and client avatar/name**. The expanded area must show only missing information: **description, client identity status, proposal form/actions, already-proposed note, dismiss action**. Do not repeat title, budget, zone, timeline, posted time, or client name/avatar in the expanded area. Use the standard chevron-down rotation for expand/collapse. Proposal status `pending` is labeled **Pendiente / Pending**; do not use the longer "Pendiente" label in this card family.

> **Oportunidades budget emphasis.** Highlight a real budget/range in the brand-tint budget container because it is a decision anchor. If the post has no budget and the copy is **A convenir / To be agreed**, render it as a plain muted row instead; do not put an unknown budget inside the same highlighted container as a concrete amount.

> **Mis propuestas separation.** A Mis propuestas card is two ideas: the opportunity/request context and **Mi propuesta**. The collapsed header owns the opportunity title, client, sent date, and status; the pro's price appears as a plain labelled row, not a tinted container. In the expanded body, group the pro's own price + message under one **Mi propuesta / My proposal** heading with flat label/value rows. Do not wrap the proposal message or accepted-client phone in separate white/ring containers; reserve containers for repeated proposal cards elsewhere.

> **Mis propuestas no repeated price (sprint 555).** The pro's **Tu precio / Your price** appears only once in each Mis propuestas card: the collapsed summary row. The expanded **Mi propuesta / My proposal** section shows the proposal message only when one exists. Do not repeat `Tu precio` again inside the expanded body.

> **Mis propuestas pending badge (sprint 556).** The `Pendiente / Pending` state in Mis propuestas is a normal waiting state, not an urgent warning. Keep the text, but style it as a calm amber status pill with a tiny amber dot, soft amber background, subtle border, and compact `text-[11px]` sizing. Accepted/declined keep the shared success/error badges.

> **Accordion expand control (sprint 557).** Dashboard cards that open/collapse use the shared `ExpandToggle` control: a compact `h-9 w-9` rounded-xl button-like indicator with a subtle border/shadow when closed, brand-blue fill when open, and a smooth 180-degree chevron rotation. Use it in Mis solicitudes, Mis publicaciones, Solicitudes recibidas, Oportunidades, and Mis propuestas so the "v" affordance looks identical across the app. Do not reintroduce one-off circular chevrons in these card families.

> **Responsive filter close X (sprint 567).** The mobile filters drawer close X is a quiet icon action, not a filled grey chip/circle. Use a transparent `h-8 w-8` rounded-lg hit area with muted icon text and only show the grey background on hover/press. Keep “Limpiar filtros” as the labelled clear action; do not make the close X visually compete with it.

> **Status filter count chips (sprint 558).** Counts in `StatusFilterTabs` render as compact rounded chips, not loose numbers. Active tab counts use a soft brand-blue chip (`#EBF5FB`, brand text, light ring); inactive counts use a neutral grey chip with a subtle hover lift. Cap long counts at `99+` so Activas/Finalizadas/Canceladas and profession filters never stretch awkwardly on mobile.

> **Status filter compact counters (sprint 588).** Status filter count chips must stay small enough that three filters fit on one row whenever practical. On mobile, use compact spacing (`gap-x-3`), `text-[13px]` tab labels, and `h-4 min-w-4 px-1 text-[10px]` count pills; scale back up from `sm`. Do not use oversized badges that push the third filter to a second row.

> **Status filter inline counters (sprint 590).** Status filter counts now render as tiny inline numbers, not pills/chips. Use `text-[11px] font-semibold tabular-nums`; active counts are brand-blue, inactive counts are muted grey. This is the preferred compact pattern when filters need to fit three items in one row.

> **Closed vs expanded card information (sprint 560).** A closed dashboard card is only for scan/triage: show the primary identity/title, status only when it adds state, and 1-2 decision facts. Move secondary context to the expanded body. Current rule: **Mis solicitudes** closed = professional + date + service; beneficiary/description/cancel reason open. **Solicitudes recibidas** closed = client + appointment date + service + urgent identity/age flags; zone/contact/cedula/note open. **Mis publicaciones** closed = publication title + proposal count/status; service/zone/published/description/proposals/actions open. **Oportunidades** closed = title + budget + service + Nueva when relevant; zone/deadline/published/description/client identity/form open. **Mis propuestas** closed = opportunity title + proposal status + your price; client/sent date/message/contact/actions open.

> **Lead icon container (sprint 562).** The leading icon on dashboard cards, landing feature cards, and key section panels is section identity, so it should use a soft brand-blue container, not neutral grey: rounded-xl/2xl, light brand border, `#EAF7FD` fill, brand-blue icon, subtle shadow. Keep metadata/field icons muted grey; only the main card/section lead icon gets this branded container.

> **Responsive drawer search/language order (sprint 581).** In the phone hamburger drawer, the smart "Busca un servicio" search is the first actionable block inside the main grouped menu for both logged-in and logged-out states. Account/access rows, Categorias/Recursos, and Idioma follow it. Mobile language buttons show only the full labels `Español` and `English`; do not show ES/EN abbreviations or checkmarks there. The selected language is communicated by active button styling only. Desktop language dropdown behavior remains separate.

> **Cancel reason label (sprint 582).** Cancellation reason inputs remain optional in behavior, but the visible field label/placeholder should say only `Motivo` / `Reason`. Do not show `(opcional)` or helper copy like "Agrega un motivo si lo deseas"; the user can infer they may leave it blank because the confirm action remains available.

> **Proposal price value color (sprint 583).** In Mis propuestas, `Tu precio` is factual content about the professional's own proposal, so the amount uses primary dark text (`#111827`), not brand blue. Keep the label muted grey and the shared money icon muted; reserve brand-blue emphasis for actionable states or opportunity budget availability.

> **Tax-included price display (sprint 642).** Real professional-entered prices/offers shown to clients must include the compact Costa Rican suffix `I.V.A.I.` (IVA incluido) in muted small text when layout allows. Use it for service prices, public card/profile prices, saved professional legacy rates, booking-modal legacy rates, and professional proposal prices. Inputs where a professional enters a price/offer also show the compact `I.V.A.I.` suffix inside the field so the pro understands the amount they publish includes VAT. Do not add it to `Consultar precio` / `Ask for price`, `A convenir`, client-entered estimated budgets, subscriptions, admin revenue, or internal payment amounts because those are not public service quotes from the professional.

> **Opportunity budget value color (sprint 587).** In Oportunidades, `Presupuesto` is card metadata, not an action. The `Banknote` icon stays muted grey like the other metadata icons, and a present budget uses primary dark text (`#111827`) instead of brand blue. The empty/consult fallback remains neutral.

> **Availability by selected place (sprint 342).** In the professional availability editor, do NOT ask for location inside every weekday row. The top **"Horario para"** control shows only fixed/base workplaces (office, workshop, clinic, etc.). **Do not show "A domicilio" or coverage zones as schedule options**: traveling to the client is a profile/search capability, not a separate set of hours. If the pro works Monday at the Grecia office and also travels within Grecia, clients may request home service inside those normal hours. The weekly card reads like a simple table: `Dia` · `Disponible` · `Horario` · `Acciones`, with open days as rows, `+Franja` plus the contextual three-dot apply action, and closed days summarized in a collapsed **"Dias cerrados (N)"** row. Date-specific changes live in a separate card below.

> **Home service on professional profiles (sprint 376).** `A domicilio` is inferred from location setup, not a manual profile toggle. A workplace with an exact map pin is a fixed/base location; a canton/province zone without a pin is coverage/client-location service. Do not show `A domicilio` as a selectable option or on `/buscar` cards. Public profile **Sobre mi** should use the broader fact label `Dónde atiende` / `Where they serve`, listing real administrative locations and `Videoconsulta` when enabled; never put "Se desplaza a tu ubicacion" or `A domicilio` where an address/location belongs.

> **Service modalities (sprint 376).** Keep delivery/attention modes simple and profile-level, not per service. The only explicit attention toggle in **Mi perfil -> Zonas o lugares donde trabajas** is `Videoconsulta`. Do not ask for `A domicilio`: it is inferred from zones without an exact map pin. Servicios should only ask for service description, price and experience; do not ask modalities there. `/buscar` stays focused and does not show modality chips. In public profile **Sobre mi**, show `Videoconsulta` inside the same `Dónde atiende` fact as physical locations so clients do not read a separate `Atención` row as "only virtual." Do not show `Presencial` or `A domicilio` as modality chips in the booking modal: physical/place context is already communicated by locations and coverage. Only show a compact `Videoconsulta disponible` informational block when the professional enabled it; exact details are coordinated on WhatsApp.

> **Professional service minimum (sprint 643).** In **Mi panel profesional -> Servicios**, a pro may remove even their last service so they can fix a wrong first selection without adding a fake replacement. Empty services is a temporary incomplete-profile state: show the empty state with `Agregar servicio`, keep profile-completion pending, set `category_id` to null, and hide the professional from public search until at least one active service exists.

> **Videoconsulta persistence (sprint 374).** Save `professionals.videoconsulta` independently from best-effort optional profile fields so a missing/unmigrated optional column can never silently drop the visible client-facing capability. The booking modal shows the videoconsulta notice when either `professional.videoconsulta` is true or legacy service data still contains a `video` modality.

> **Videoconsulta category gate (sprint 604).** `Videoconsulta` is profile-level, but it is NOT universal. Show the toggle in **Mi perfil** only when the professional has at least one category in `VIDEO_CONSULT_CATEGORY_IDS`; otherwise save `professionals.videoconsulta` as false. In **Sobre mi**, list `Videoconsulta` under `Donde atiende` only when the pro enabled it and their profile has a video-compatible category. In **Solicitar servicio**, show the `Videoconsulta disponible` note only when the selected/effective booking category supports it. User-suggested categories stay admin-reviewed; the admin surface should use `classifySuggestedCategory()` to flag `Revisar salud` and `Revisar videoconsulta` as review hints, never as automatic approval.

> **Admin category flags (sprint 605, updated).** Admin -> Servicios (`/admin/servicios`) is a three-surface catalog manager: **Servicios**, **Secciones**, and **Sugerencias**. Admins can mark each service as `Salud` (drives DOB / "para otra persona" in direct bookings and open published requests) and `Videoconsulta` (drives the profile toggle and booking notice). The app still has code defaults for resilience, but Supabase `categories.es_salud` and `categories.supports_videoconsulta` are the operational source when loaded. User suggestions are semi-automatic: the app proposes Spanish cleanup, English label, section, and flags through `classifySuggestedCategory()` / search matching, but an admin must confirm or edit before approval.

> **Admin services naming and deletion (sprint 608, updated).** In admin-facing copy, call the catalog `Servicios` / `Catalogo de servicios`; `categorias` remains an internal route/table concept. Admins may add, edit, move, and remove every active service. Removing a fixed/base service hides it (`categories.is_hidden`) instead of physically deleting it so historical profiles, requests, URLs, and analytics do not break.

> **Admin service English names (sprint 611, updated).** Every admin-added or approved custom service must have an English display name stored in `categories.name_en`. The admin does not need to translate manually: the app auto-generates `name_en` from the Spanish label and keeps updating it while the admin has not manually edited the English field. Pending user suggestions must hydrate the English field through the translation API as soon as the suggestions list loads, so the admin reviews Spanish + English together before approval. Once the admin types in English, that manual value wins. Public EN rendering should use `labelEn` for custom services and fall back to generated English before ever showing a slug. Server-side generation uses `suggestEnglishServiceLabel()`: local dictionary first/fallback, Google Cloud Translation v3 when `GOOGLE_TRANSLATE_SERVICE_ACCOUNT_JSON` + `GOOGLE_CLOUD_PROJECT_ID` are configured, or Google Cloud Translation API key fallback when only `GOOGLE_TRANSLATE_API_KEY` is configured. Translation credentials are server-only; never expose them as `NEXT_PUBLIC_*`. The API must not silently drop `name_en` on save unless the DB explicitly lacks that column.

> **Bilingual service suggestions (sprint 620).** Public service suggestions must preserve the user's language while keeping Spanish as the canonical catalog label. Suggestions sent from `/es` store `category_suggestions.label` in Spanish and auto-fill `categories.name_en` through ES -> EN translation. Suggestions sent from `/en` keep the user's English text as `categories.name_en` and auto-fill `category_suggestions.label` / `suggested_name` through EN -> ES translation. Pending suggestion rows may create a hidden `categories` draft only to carry `name_en`; never let that draft become public until admin approval, and never overwrite or hide an already-active service.

> **Bidirectional admin translation (sprint 621).** Admin service and section forms must work from either language. If the admin types Spanish, auto-fill English; if the admin types English first, auto-fill Spanish before saving. Server endpoints must also accept English-only creation and translate it to the canonical Spanish `name` / `label` before slugging. Google Translation should auto-detect the source language instead of forcing `es` or `en`, because admin-entered service names are short and can arrive in either language.

> **Service name normalization (sprint 622).** Every service/section name created from admin or public suggestions must be reviewed by Cloud Translation in both Spanish and English before saving, regardless of what language the user/admin typed. Store the Spanish canonical label in sentence case (`Primera letra en mayúscula`, rest lowercase) and store the English label the same way. Do not preserve all-caps/user casing in catalog names unless a future explicit acronym allow-list is added.

> **Admin service deletion visibility (sprint 615, updated).** The admin services catalog must make deletion discoverable without noisy taxonomy jargon. Do not show confusing labels like `Base protegido`, `Otras categorias agregado en`, or raw internal status text. Use simple filters (`Todos`, `Agregados`, `Salud`, `Video`) and a clear `Eliminar` action for rows; deletion means hide for base items and delete/hide for custom items depending on DB safety.

> **Admin full service management (sprint 617).** Admins can edit every service name, move every service to a catalog section, and remove every service from the active catalog. Removing a fixed/base service must hide it (`categories.is_hidden`) instead of physically deleting it so historical profiles, requests, and URLs do not break. New services must get a `group_id`: infer it when possible, but always expose a manual section selector in admin. Do not show `Otras categorias` as the public/default admin group for new services when a real section can be assigned.

> **Admin service sections (sprint 618, updated).** The admin catalog owns **Servicios**, **Secciones**, and **Sugerencias** as separate tabs. A new section is stored in `category_groups` with Spanish + English labels, order, icon key, and hidden flag; public catalog surfaces load these groups dynamically through `/api/categories/approved` and fall back to the fixed taxonomy if the table is not present yet. Service and section names must expose editable Spanish and English fields. Auto-translation is only a suggestion/fill-in, never the final authority.

> **Public catalog source (sprint 619).** Public service surfaces (`/servicios`, navbar Servicios dropdown, shared service pickers, and search helpers) must load custom services from the active `categories` table, not only from `category_suggestions`. `category_suggestions` is a review inbox/compatibility source; once a service is active in admin, `categories` is the operational source for label, English label, section, hidden state, health flag, and videoconsulta flag.

> **Public service sections (sprint 623).** Admin-created service sections from `category_groups` must appear in public browsing surfaces (`/servicios`, desktop navbar Servicios dropdown, and shared category pickers) even when the section has zero services. Empty sections show a quiet empty state instead of disappearing, so the admin can verify the taxonomy structure immediately after creating it.

> **Service search by section (sprint 624).** Any service search surface must match both service names/keywords and section names. When a user types a section such as `Tecnologia` / `Technology`, results should be the services inside that section, not the section as a selectable service. Exact section intent may route to the first service in that section only as a practical fallback for existing `/buscar?categoria=` flows; do not create fake service IDs for sections.

> **Navbar service search context (sprint 625).** In the desktop navbar Servicios dropdown, typed search results must show their section context together with the service name. A result should read like a catalog path (`Seccion` + `Servicio`) rather than a lone service word, so users understand where the result lives in the service taxonomy.

> **Navbar service search structure (sprint 626).** The desktop navbar Servicios dropdown must keep the same mental model as `/servicios` when searching: filter the catalog by section and show the matching services inside the selected section. Do not collapse search results into a flat list of service words.

> **Public services naming (sprint 609).** Public navigation and suggestion copy should say `Servicios`, not `Categorias`: users search for services, while category is an internal taxonomy concept. Navbar dropdowns, `/servicios`, and no-match CTAs should say `No ves tu servicio?` / `Suggest a service`. When a search has exactly one service match, render a single direct result row; do not show both a service row and a category/group field that makes the result look duplicated.

> **Services catalog suggestion CTA (sprint 612).** Do not add a separate bottom `No encuentras tu servicio?` section on `/servicios`; it duplicates the existing suggestion flow and makes the catalog feel heavier. Keep the page focused on browsing/searching services. Suggestion entry points should live where the user is actively searching or adding a professional service.

> **Services catalog route and layout (sprint 613).** The public catalog URL is `/servicios`; `/categorias` is only a backwards-compatible redirect. The catalog should behave like a practical directory: integrated search in the header, compact group navigation, and one continuous service surface with evenly sized rows/chips instead of uneven repeated cards.

> **Services catalog quick paths (sprint 614, updated).** `/servicios` should NOT include a separate `Servicios populares` rail or summary chips such as `12 grupos / 109 servicios`. The catalog already has search + section browsing; extra counters and popular shortcuts make the page feel heavier without improving the main task.

> **Services catalog section-first redesign (sprint 616, updated).** `/servicios` should feel like a modern app directory: short intro, then one explorer surface. The search bar belongs INSIDE that explorer surface as the first row, visually connected to the results/list below, not floating as a separate card. Desktop uses a left section sidebar and a right service list; mobile uses a horizontally scrollable section rail above the service list. Avoid large repeated section tiles or a separate popular rail. Search mode is the exception: typed queries show direct service results across all sections with the section label visible.

> **Search card mobile identity row (sprint 363).** On phone, the professional/company name has priority over compactness: do not clamp the primary or secondary name in `/buscar` mobile cards. Keep the favorite bookmark high in the card and keep the price column narrow/right-aligned so long Costa Rican names can wrap naturally. Desktop may keep a tighter one-line header.

> **Search mobile map sheet (sprint 364).** The `/buscar` mobile bottom sheet may scroll its card list internally, but it must never expand high enough to cover the map controls or filter trigger. Keep a visible map-control band below the navbar at all snap points; users should not be able to drag/scroll the sheet into a state where filters or map actions disappear.

> **Services index page (sprint 375, updated).** `/servicios` should feel like a modern service directory: compact intro, connected search row, app-like section explorer, and one continuous service surface. Do not show summary counters like `12 grupos / 109 servicios`; they add visual noise without helping users choose. Search results render directly inside the same explorer surface, not in a detached card or floating dropdown. Avoid uneven card grids and loose chip clouds; every service link should read as a clean tappable row with consistent height and a chevron. The suggestion flow belongs inside the search experience: show `No ves tu servicio?` when there are no matches and as a compact contextual footer after filtered results, not as a detached bottom marketing section.

> **Services page search/menu connection (sprint 607, updated).** On `/servicios`, the search input and service menu are one connected surface. Typing filters the desktop side menu and mobile rail to matching sections, and the main content keeps the selected matching section on the right. Do not switch to a disconnected flat grid. Search mode should still show the matching section labels, the services inside the selected section, and a clear contextual way to suggest a missing service.

> **Services catalog visual weight (sprint 610).** `/servicios` is the public services catalog, so it should read as one continuous directory, not a stack of unrelated cards. Use one compact search hero, a low-chrome sticky menu/rail, and service lists with soft dividers. Avoid wrapping the sidebar, search summary, every section, and suggestion block in separate heavy containers; reserve borders for the actual list surfaces and use simple spacing for section transitions.

> **Mis publicaciones publish CTA placement (sprint 584).** On desktop, `Publicar una solicitud` belongs in the section header row, inline with the `Mis publicaciones` subtitle (`Solicitudes que publicaste para recibir propuestas.`), aligned to the right. Do not place a second desktop CTA above the filters. On mobile, keep publish actions thumb-reachable (FAB/empty-state CTA) rather than forcing the full button into the header.

> **Mis publicaciones proposal verification badge (sprint 585).** In each proposal card shown to the client, display the proposing professional's verification status next to their name as a small `Verificado` / `Sin verificar` badge. This is the right trust signal for choosing a proposal. Never expose the professional's or client's cédula/ID number in this proposal list.

> **Proposal verification badge style (sprint 589).** The proposal-card professional verification indicator must visually match the panel badge under the user's name: verified = solid brand-blue `Badge variant="verified"`; not verified = grey rounded pill with `border-[#e5e7eb] bg-[#f3f4f6] text-[#6b7280]`. Do not use success-green, muted badge variants, or one-off tiny badge sizing for this state.

> **Navbar trigger active state (sprint 576).** Top-level navbar navigation should NOT stay visually marked just because the current URL matches it. Categorias, Recursos, Mi panel, Notificaciones, and responsive drawer rows use neutral text by default with hover/focus feedback only. Opening a dropdown may rotate the chevron and use a temporary hover-like background while the dropdown is open, but do not keep a blue underline, route-active tint, or left rail after navigation. Language is the exception because it is a selector and should show the active locale.

> **Navbar category search localization (sprint 563).** In the desktop Categorias mega-menu, typing a service keeps results INSIDE the new category surface and shows each matched service with its parent category group. Do not return to a flat unlabeled grid or a separate floating autocomplete; the user must understand where the service lives in the taxonomy before clicking.

> **Category pickers: sections first, options second (sprint 532).** Any category/service picker that exposes the full catalog must use the two-level pattern: **level 1 = catalog sections only** (section name + option count + chevron), **level 2 = options for the opened section only** with a clear **Volver / Back** row. This applies consistently to the navbar Servicios dropdown, shared `CategorySearch` fields (Publicar solicitud, registration/search filters), and the pro Agregar servicio/profesion picker. `/servicios` is the exception because it is a directory page, so it may show selectable section tiles plus the selected section's services. Search mode may show direct filtered matches after the user types. Suggestion CTAs belong in active picker/search flows, not as a bottom section of `/servicios`. Navigation must be keyboard-accessible through native buttons/links (Tab + Enter/Space), with Escape/click-outside preserved in dropdown hosts, and must fit ~360px without truncating section names.

> **Category picker focus (sprint 536).** Section rows in shared category pickers use `focus-visible`, not plain `focus`, for the blue ring. Mouse/touch clicks should open the section without leaving a persistent border/hover-looking mark; keyboard Tab navigation must still show a visible focus ring.

> **Logged-out responsive drawer (sprint 565).** When no session is active, the mobile drawer still uses the modern grouped-menu structure: session actions get their own top block, public browsing rows stay in the navigation block, and the language selector remains the chip block. Do not mix Ofrecer/Iniciar sesión at the bottom of the navigation rows; that makes the closed-session menu feel less intentional than the logged-in one.

> **Language selector (sprint 564).** The language control is a quiet utility, not a primary nav item. Desktop uses a compact globe + current locale pill with a small two-option dropdown showing label, code, and a check on the active language. Mobile uses the same language block inside the drawer: muted “Idioma/Language” label with globe, then two equal chips. Do not use a loose text pair or an empty heading/container.

> **Profile chip pickers (sprint 626).** Profile multi-select fields such as Idiomas/Aseguradoras should open as a picker first on mobile, not immediately focus a text input. Show common options without bringing up the keyboard; keep search inside the dropdown for filtering only. Health-only fields must read the dynamic catalog flags (`es_salud`) so admin-added health services expose the same insurance options as fixed health services.

> **Responsive navbar parity (sprint 265).** On phone, the hamburger drawer mirrors the desktop navbar, not the account dropdown: search at the top, then **Categorias**, the **Recursos** links, and the same session actions shown in the desktop navbar, each as icon-led rows. Account-specific quick links belong to the profile-photo dropdown. The profile-photo dropdown must expose the same options on mobile and desktop; do not hide mode-specific quick links on phone and do not add mobile-only language controls inside that dropdown. For logged-out users, the session-action rows have **NO section heading and NO separate field/container**; render **Ofrecer mis servicios / Ingresar** as normal rows in the same navigation list, and never show **Mi cuenta / My account** because no account is active yet.

> **Responsive navbar drawer surface (sprint 576).** The phone hamburger drawer uses grouped app-menu surfaces, not divider-only lists: drawer background `#f8fafc`, white rounded-2xl groups with subtle border/shadow, search in its own white group, and navigation rows at least 48px tall. Drawer navigation rows stay neutral after navigation; do not show route-active brand-blue backgrounds or left rails. Keep "Ofrecer servicios" as a primary blue row for logged-out users. For logged-in users, show a compact account identity block (avatar, name, email) above the account rows so the menu feels like the same account surface as desktop.

> **Responsive navbar drawer order (sprint 553).** On phone, the hamburger drawer is one attached white app-menu tray with internal dividers, not scattered floating cards. For logged-in users, put the account block first because it contains the highest-intent actions: avatar/name/email, **Mi panel**, **Notificaciones**, and **Ofrecer servicios** only when the user is not yet a pro. Then show the service search, Categorias/Recursos, and Idioma. For logged-out users, keep the same grouped mobile-menu language as logged-in: first the session actions as their own compact block (**Ofrecer mis servicios** primary, **Ingresar** secondary), then service search, Categorias/Recursos, and Idioma. Do not add empty headings such as "Acceso" or "Mi cuenta"; rows and icons carry the meaning.

> **Queued UI fixes standard (sprint 535).** In **Solicitudes recibidas**, the client note belongs inside the same details block as service/location/contact, separated by a divider and label, not as a separate bordered/accent card; truncate the preview early enough (`~2` lines) that "Ver mas" reveals meaningful text. On the public professional profile, **Servicios** cards are text-only: no catalog images, no reserved media space, just service, short description/fallback, price, and CTA. **Casos de exito** uses one larger card per row on mobile and desktop; cards should be easy to inspect, with room for photo, title, description, recipient/date, and likes. The pro editor limit is **3 cases per profession/service**, not total. On `/categorias`, click/touch expansion must not leave a persistent focus-looking border; use `focus-visible` so keyboard users still get a clear ring.

> **Client identity for open postings (sprint 531).** "Publicar solicitud" now requires **Número de identificación** via the shared `CedulaInput`. It uses `lib/cedula` for format and the same padrón-backed verifier (`getIdentityVerifier().lookup`) as the professional verification flow; `runIdentityVerification` is intentionally NOT reused because it is professional-row-specific. Store the account-level result on `profiles.client_identity_status` (`verified` / `pending` / `unverified`) and copy a snapshot to `projects.client_identity_status`; never store or show the cédula number on the project. In **Oportunidades → Información del cliente**, show the result as **plain text only** ("Identidad verificada" / "Identidad pendiente de revisión" / "Identidad no verificada"), no badge, no tinted box, no extra icon. If the ID cannot be confirmed or remains pending, the client must see a clear inline message after publishing.

> **Health open postings beneficiary rule.** When a client publishes an open request and the selected service is marked `Salud`, show a compact `¿Para quién es?` choice in the publish modal. `Para mí` adds no extra fields. `Para otra persona` asks only for the person's full name and date of birth, mirroring the direct-booking beneficiary model and storing `projects.for_someone_else`, `beneficiary_name`, `beneficiary_dob`, and `beneficiary_is_minor`. Show this context only in opened Mis publicaciones/Oportunidades cards; keep closed cards scan-first.

> **Account identity is shared across client/pro modes (sprint 537).** `profiles.cedula` and the official `profiles.full_name` are account-level identity fields, not per-mode fields. Any flow that collects cédula (Publicar solicitud, booking, professional verification/registration) must reuse/preserve the same profile values. In Publicar solicitud, typing a national cédula should lookup the padrón before submit and show the same amber warning used by Solicitar servicio: "La cédula ingresada pertenece a {nombre}. Al confirmar, tu cuenta usará este nombre oficial. Usa únicamente tu propia cédula." Keep the "No tengo cédula" escape path consistent with booking; if `profiles.cedula` already exists, do not show the cédula field again and submit the saved account cédula automatically. The server still verifies again and must not overwrite an existing good profile name with stale auth metadata.

> **FULL Airbnb mode model (IMPLEMENTED — sprint 406).** ContrataCR uses one account with two worlds — **Modo cliente** ("use" / busco servicios) and **Modo profesional** ("offer" / ofrezco servicios) — and a **single switch that flips the ENTIRE experience** (navigation + content + notifications), Airbnb-style. There is **no in-panel segmented toggle and no coexisting sections**.

> **Panel navigation — mobile bottom nav bar (sprint 416).** On phones the panel uses a **native-app-style fixed bottom navigation bar** (`lg:hidden fixed bottom-0`), NOT the sidebar (which is `hidden lg:block` on desktop). It's a polished **icon + label** tab bar (Instagram/WhatsApp style, sprint 417) reusing the panel's `TAB_ICONS`; the item set is **capped per mode so it ALWAYS fits — never a horizontal scroll**, and BOTH modes use **3 sections + Notificaciones + Más = 5 slots** (sprint 432): **offer = Perfil · Solicitudes recibidas · Oportunidades · Notificaciones · Más**; **use = Perfil · Mis solicitudes · Mis publicaciones · Notificaciones · Más** (`MOBILE_PRIMARY`). **Notificaciones has a DEDICATED slot with its own mode-scoped unread badge** (brand-blue `#009FD9` white, `9+` cap, on the Bell icon) — so **"Más" carries NO notification dot** (it just opens the overflow; Soporte's unread still shows as its row badge inside the sheet). Everything else lives in "Más" (offer: Profesiones · Casos de éxito · Disponibilidad · Verificación · Suscripción · Soporte · Cuenta; use: Favoritos · Soporte · Cuenta). `primaryTabs` filters `MOBILE_PRIMARY[mode]` against `[...modeTabs, ...SHARED_TABS]` so the shared `notifications` tab is allowed into the bar. Any tab placed in `MOBILE_PRIMARY` MUST have a `proPanel.bottomNav.<tab>` short label (the bar renders `t(\`bottomNav.${tab}\`)` — a missing key throws at runtime) and a `TAB_ICONS` entry. **The "Más" bottom-sheet has full dismissal (sprint 438):** a tappable **drag-handle** (top-center) + a clear **X button (top-right of the header)** + **tap-outside** scrim — all call `setMoreOpen(false)` (`proPanel.bottomNav.close`). Use this same pattern (handle + X + scrim) for any bottom-sheet.

> **Panel context chip (sprint 593).** Dashboard content headers must explicitly show `Panel cliente` or `Panel profesional` next to the section title on mobile and desktop. Do not place this above the account name/photo. Offer-only tabs always show professional, use-only tabs always show client, and shared tabs use the current mode.

> **Confirm modals + per-card action hierarchy (sprint 434/437/438).** Destructive/confirm actions use a **clean on-brand modal**, NEVER `window.prompt`/`window.confirm`: a `fixed inset-0 z-[100] flex items-end sm:items-center` overlay + a `w-full sm:max-w-sm bg-white rounded-t-2xl sm:rounded-2xl p-5` card — **title** (bold) + brief **body** + a 2-button row "Volver" (outline) / destructive action (red `bg-red-600`, `loading` state). Used by: client cancel booking ("¿Cancelar esta solicitud?", optional note), delete publicación ("¿Eliminar esta solicitud?" / "Esta acción no se puede deshacer."), and **report** (shared `ReportModal` — title + one-line body + ONE required free-text textarea, NO suggested reason chips, + "Volver" / red "Reportar"; used for both reportar-cliente and reportar-profesional; portaled to `document.body` so it opens above responsive panel surfaces). **Per-card action hierarchy (Mis publicaciones):** the **create CTA "Publicar una solicitud" is a PRIMARY** at the TOP of the section (full-width mobile, `rounded-full px-5`); per-card management = **outline pills** (`rounded-full px-4`, Cancelar red); the destructive **Eliminar is a quiet text link** that opens the delete modal. Keep create-primary-at-top, manage-secondary-per-card. The active item is **brand-blue `#009FD9`** (icon + label); **"Más"** (a `MoreHorizontal` icon) opens a bottom sheet (scrim + drag handle) listing the remaining sections as icon + label + unread badge (a brand dot sits on "Más" when an overflow section has unread). NOTE: a bottom tab bar is the ONE place icons are the modern standard — this does NOT loosen the "zero icons" rule for request-card content. **The panel footer is hidden on mobile** (`hidden lg:block`) so the bar takes its place; content gets `pb-24 lg:pb-8`. Desktop keeps the left-rail sidebar unchanged. **The switch is a SEGMENTED CONTROL and lives ONLY inside "Mi panel" — NEVER in the navbar or the responsive menu (sprint 518, SUPERSEDES sprint 424).** The right pattern for switching between two views/contexts is a **segmented control** (a.k.a. content switcher), not a button or an on/off toggle: both modes shown side by side, the active one filled, you tap the other to switch the whole experience. The shared **`ModeSwitcher` component (`@/components/ui/mode-switcher`)** renders two segments — **"Cliente" / "Profesional"** (`header.modeClientShort`/`modeProShort`) — in a `rounded-full bg-[#f3f4f6] p-0.5` track; the **active segment is filled brand-blue `#008ce0` with white text + a soft shadow**, the inactive one is **muted grey `#6b7280` (hover → navy `#162543`) but clearly tappable** (`transition-all duration-200`). Accessible: `role="tablist"`/`role="tab"` + `aria-selected`, `tabIndex` roving, **ArrowLeft/ArrowRight** operable; `aria-label` via `header.modeSwitchAria`. **Placement (sprint 518): it sits in the PANEL HEADER's right block** (above the "Ver perfil como cliente" action; stacks full-width on mobile, right-aligned on desktop), wired to a panel `handleSwitchMode(next)` that `setMode`s AND lands on the destination mode's main tab (offer→`bookings`, use→`sent_bookings`) so a switch from ANY section sticks. It was **REMOVED from the navbar bar, the account-menu dropdown, and the mobile drawer** — rationale: anyone switching goes to their panel anyway, and removing it freed navbar room to **restore the "ContrataCR" wordmark on mobile** (the mobile logo now compacts to the mark ONLY when the `/buscar` inline search occupies the row). Only for accounts with both modes (`isProvider`); a client-only account never sees it. **The mode control carries NO notification badge (sprint 425)** — context/view switchers stay clean; notifications live in the bell (modern-app practice).

> **Mis publicaciones proposal count.** The proposals count, including **0 propuestas / 0 proposals**, is simple card metadata with a small `Users` icon and muted text. It must NOT sit in its own colored container/badge unless there is a real actionable alert state; zero activity should not become the visual focus of the card.

> **Solicitudes recibidas appointment date.** In received request cards, the appointment date/time (for example `Jue, 25 jun · 11:00 am`) is metadata, not a standalone callout. Show it as a flat row with a small calendar/clock icon, aligned with service and location rows. Do NOT put it in a colored/bordered container; the client identity/avatar and the request status should carry the card hierarchy.

> **Identity verification is account-level.** A person can use ContrataCR as client and professional, but their identification/status belongs to the single `profiles` account, not to separate modes. Admin user search counts one `profiles` row as one user even if it has a `professionals` row. Verifying a professional identity must also set `profiles.client_identity_status` and open `projects.client_identity_status` to verified; revoking/rejecting/reverting it must remove the saved `profiles.cedula`, clear auth cedula metadata, and update both client/pro surfaces so the next booking or posted request asks for identification again. Converting an existing client to professional must reuse the stored account identification when present and never ask for it again or overwrite it with null.

> **Client verification badge source (sprint 627).** The `Verificado` badge in Panel cliente must be driven by `profiles.client_identity_status === "verified"` (plus a valid saved national identification), never by the mere presence of `profiles.cedula`. Revoking or reverting verification from admin must remove the client-side badge on the next profile refresh and force the next client identity flow to ask again.

> **Language switcher — discreet, NOT a segmented control (sprint 425).** To avoid two competing identical toggles next to the MODE segmented control, the language switch uses a **different, quieter** treatment and is **subordinate** to the mode switch. **Desktop:** `LanguageMenu` — a discreet **globe + current code ("ES"/"EN") + chevron** button in the navbar right-actions that opens a tiny dropdown (`Español` / `English`, the active one brand-blue `#009FD9` + a `Check`); quiet grey, no pill track. **Mobile:** language is **NOT in the header** (kept lean: logo + "ContrataCR" wordmark + bell + menu — the mode switch moved to the panel in sprint 518) — it lives in the **profile menu + hamburger drawer** instead (changed rarely, so it doesn't need header space): `LanguageInline` — a discreet **plain-text pair "Español · English"** (current = brand-blue bold, other = grey), distinct from any pill/segmented look. It appears in the account-menu dropdown (`lg:hidden`, under "Idioma" with a globe) and the drawer "Idioma" row. Shared `useSwitchLang` helper persists the choice (localStorage `contratacr_lang` + the `NEXT_LOCALE` cookie) and `router.replace`s the locale. Always clearly shows the current language. Mode is GLOBAL and persisted (`useMode` → localStorage `contratacr_mode`, synced via the `ccr:mode-changed` event); a **non-provider is always clamped to "use"**. **A client-only account shows NO "modo" wording at all (sprint 411)** — the "Modo …" label/eyebrow + the switch chip only appear when the account has BOTH modes (`isProvider`); a client-only user sees just their name, "Mi cuenta" headings, and the **"Ofrecer mis servicios" invitation at the END of "Mi perfil"** (`BasicProfileSection`, gated on `!canOffer`) — that's how they discover/activate offering. The panel shows a quiet **"Modo …"** eyebrow and reads the global mode; deep-link tabs (notification hrefs / quick links) force + persist their mode. **Notifications are PER-MODE** (`notificationInMode(type, mode)`: professional→offer, client→use, support/unknown→both): the navbar bell + the panel Notificaciones tab show ONLY the active mode's items, so the old ambiguous "Ver todas" is gone (one unambiguous "Ver todas" → the current mode). **Other-mode awareness without switching:** a small unread-count badge on the "Cambiar a modo …" item surfaces the OTHER world's pending count (active-mode badge = own + account-level; other-mode badge = the other world's pure count). **Emails: still in-app + WhatsApp ONLY — none added** (Brevo free-plan burst risk; revisit on a paid plan: propuesta→client first, then an oportunidad daily digest).

> **Logo on dark surfaces (sprint 293).** Use `ContrataCRLogo tone="dark"` on dark chrome such as the public footer and admin shell. Do not put the logo inside a white chip/container there; the dark tone uses the transparent dark mark asset (`logo-mark-dark.png`) and keeps the wordmark legible while preserving the clean dark footer. Light surfaces use the transparent normal mark (`logo-mark-transparent.png`) so the old white exported canvas never shows.

> **Support case references (sprint 294).** Every support ticket shown to users or admins must display a short stable reference (`Caso #123456` / `Case #123456`) near the subject, both in the ticket list and the opened thread. The reference is for human conversation with support; do not expose the full UUID in the UI.

> **Future subscriptions/payments (sprint 301).** Payments are OFF for users until `NEXT_PUBLIC_PAYMENTS_ENABLED=true`. The planned launch is **card-only automatic recurring billing**, not SINPE/manual comprobantes. Pricing: **CRC 3,000 monthly** and **CRC 30,000 annual** (pay 10 months, save 2). Special launch benefits are INTERNAL admin-granted promotional subscription periods, not roles and not public copy: existing professionals can get **2 free months**; early collaborators can get **12 free months**. Do not mention these benefits in the public/pro-facing plan UI. Do not add public SINPE/manual payment UI unless the business decision explicitly changes.

> **Solicitudes AUTO-CONFIRM (implemented — sprint 390).** A solicitud confirms immediately and holds the slot — there is **NO manual "accept" step**. The professional manages exceptions from their panel: **cancel-with-reason** (a single **free-text "Motivo (opcional)"** — preset reason chips were removed sprint 436 → the client is notified with the motivo if given, the slot frees) and **WhatsApp** (always available). **Reschedule model — only the CLIENT reschedules (sprint 433):** the appointment is the client's, so the **pro does NOT have a "Reprogramar" action** (moving the client's time unilaterally is invasive for a connector) — the pro cancels-with-reason and coordinates any new time via WhatsApp (propose, not impose). The **CLIENT reschedules** via a `RescheduleModal` (`components/booking/reschedule-modal.tsx`, opened from their "Mis solicitudes" card): it shows the SAME pro's availability (weekly `availability` + explicit `availability_slots` − `blocked_dates` − taken slots − the 15-min CR lead, identical logic to the booking modal), the client picks a new slot → `PATCH /api/bookings { id, status:"confirmed", scheduledDate, scheduledTime }` **moves the booking** (old slot freed since it's only "taken" while the row sits on it; new slot occupied, **atomic** via the migration-069 unique index → a raced slot returns 409 "Ese horario ya está reservado"). On reschedule, `notifyBookingRescheduled` notifies the **PROFESSIONAL** ("Cita reprogramada" + the new time, in-app + WhatsApp); `booking_rescheduled` is a **PRO_TYPES** notification (shows in offer mode, links to `?tab=bookings`). If the pro's availability is private/empty, the modal shows a "coordina por WhatsApp" note instead. **Cancel panels:** **CLIENT cancel is low-friction** — an inline panel inside the expanded Mis solicitudes card ("¿Cancelar esta solicitud?" + "Se liberará el horario y se le avisará al profesional.") with an **OPTIONAL** note field (no forced reason; `window.prompt` and the later modal were removed) → "Volver" / "Sí, cancelar" (red). **PRO cancel keeps a reason** — the inline cancel-with-reason panel is a single **free-text "Motivo (opcional)"** textarea (sprint 436 removed the preset chips "No tengo disponibilidad"/"No es mi área de servicio"/"El cliente no responde" AND the "Se le avisa al cliente con el motivo…" helper line — minimal text), optional but available, so the client is told why. When the **pro** cancels, the client's booking moves to **Canceladas showing a red "Cancelada por el profesional" + Motivo** block (reads `cancelled_by`/`cancel_reason`), the slot frees, and the client is notified — they simply **re-book** (the card name links to the pro's profile) or coordinate via WhatsApp. No complex per-reason flows, and **no reschedule button in Canceladas** (reschedule is gated to ACTIVE statuses only). **Reason is OPTIONAL for BOTH sides** — neither cancel confirm button is blocked on an empty reason (`cancelReason: motivo || undefined`); the pro panel shows preset chips + "Motivo (opcional)", the client inline panel a "Motivo (opcional)" field. **Cancel notifications are actor-correct (sprint 435):** `notifyBookingStatusChange` always notifies the CLIENT, so the `api/bookings` PATCH only fires it for a **pro-initiated** cancel (`status === "cancelled" && isOwnerPro`); a **client-initiated** cancel instead notifies the **PRO** ("El cliente canceló la solicitud" + motivo, `booking_update`, links to `?tab=bookings`) — never a confusing self-notification to the client. The "sin cédula" client shows a **"Sin verificar"** chip so the pro can decide — auto-confirm is NEVER blocked for unverified clients (no separate approval flow). **Atomic double-booking guard:** `api/bookings` keeps the fast-path clash check AND relies on the **migration-069 partial unique index** `(professional_id, scheduled_date, scheduled_time) WHERE status IN ('pending','confirmed','in_progress') AND scheduled_date IS NOT NULL AND scheduled_time IS NOT NULL` — a true race's loser hits 23505 → 409 "Ese horario acaba de ser reservado". Cancel (by either side) frees the slot automatically. "Confirmada" means **"horario reservado — coordina por WhatsApp"**, not "the pro approved you" (the success screen + copy say so). **Solicitud status tabs dropped "Pendientes"** → **Confirmadas · Finalizadas · Canceladas** (default Confirmadas); proyectos/propuestas keep the 4-bucket set (Pendientes = open / awaiting decision is still real there).

> **"Solicitudes recibidas" card — scan-first, minimal identity (sprint 405).** Each request card shows only what the pro acts on: **who** (client name + the **booker's cédula** formatted `X-XXXX-XXXX` via `formatId` so the pro can confirm who reserved — always `client_cedula`, never the beneficiary; when there's no cédula on file, the **"Sin verificar"** pill speaks instead of a blank, sprint 412), **when** (the bold appointment date/time is the scan anchor — no "Fecha de la cita" label; the muted "Solicitada …" in the header marks the request date), **what** (categoría · lugar + the client's note), and the **actions** (WhatsApp · Reprogramar · Cancelar/liberar · Marcar realizado · Reportar). **"Para mí" (the common case) is one compact person** — name + age badge (only for a minor/adulto mayor) + phone + WhatsApp; NO beneficiary section, NO DOB row, NO cédula number. **"Para otra persona" (health minority) is a single column, not a two-column split** — the PATIENT is the headline (name + age badge + DOB) under a small "La cita es para" eyebrow, then a one-line **"Reservado por {nombre} · {teléfono}"** + WhatsApp (the requester is the only contact; the beneficiary has no phone/cédula). **Action row (sprint 408):** all four actions live in ONE group with hierarchy — **primaries** WhatsApp (green) + Marcar completado (brand filled), **secondaries** Reprogramar + Cancelar (outline, Cancelar red). Responsive: `flex flex-col sm:flex-row sm:flex-wrap gap-2` — mobile stacks the two primaries full-width and puts the two secondaries in a 2-col row (`grid grid-cols-2 sm:contents`), desktop is one wrapping row. WhatsApp shows for any booking; the management actions for ACTIVE bookings only.

> **Status filter tabs (sprint 430, supersedes 419/413/408).** `StatusFilterTabs` (shared by Solicitudes recibidas, Mis solicitudes, Mis publicaciones, Oportunidades/Mis propuestas) is a **clean wrapping row of pills** — `flex flex-wrap gap-2`, active pill = brand-blue fill, inactive = white/border. **ONE unified filter set for ALL lifecycles: `Activas · Finalizadas · Canceladas`** (bucket ids `activas`/`finalizadas`/`canceladas`). **"Pendientes" is GONE** and **"Confirmadas" was renamed → "Activas"** (sprint 430): bookings AUTO-CONFIRM and a published solicitud is simply live until it finishes, so there is no "pending" stage — `activas` = any live/ongoing item (a confirmed booking, an open/assigned project, a sent proposal awaiting a decision). Buckets: `solicitudBucket`/`proyectoBucket`/`proposalBucket` all map cancelled→canceladas, completed/past→finalizadas, everything-else→activas. Sub-states still show an in-card badge when they add info, but the bucket's DEFAULT/expected status is treated as redundant and hidden: in **Mis publicaciones**, `PROYECTO_PRIMARY.activas = ["open"]` so an **open project shows NO "Abierto" badge** (it's the default active state), while **Asignado** (in_progress) / **Esperando confirmación** (awaiting) DO show (sprint 437); a pending proposal still shows "En espera". **Readability (sprint 437):** card text uses `#111827` (primary), `#374151` (values), `#6b7280` (labels/sublines/tertiary) — **never the washed-out `#9ca3af` for readable text** (it's reserved for input placeholders only). **Shared client/pro action buttons are IDENTICAL (sprint 437):** an action that exists on both sides (Cancelar, Reportar, WhatsApp, Marcar completado) uses the SAME treatment everywhere — **Cancelar = red-outline pill** `rounded-full px-4 text-red-600 border-red-200 hover:bg-red-50`, **WhatsApp = green pill** `variant="whatsapp" rounded-full px-4`, **Reportar = quiet text link** `self-start text-xs font-semibold text-[#6b7280] hover:text-red-500` — laid out as primary-full-width-on-mobile + 2-col secondaries + one wrapping desktop row (the client's "Mis solicitudes" card was restructured from a right-hand button column into this footer to match the pro's Solicitudes recibidas). Default filter everywhere = `activas`. **NO per-filter count badges** (a count on every filter is clutter; reserve count badges for the bell). **Count-badge colour rule:** every count badge in the app is **solid brand-blue `#009FD9` white** — NEVER red.

> **User text must never break a card's width (sprint 444).** EVERY element that renders user-entered text (descriptions, notes, proposal messages) gets **`break-words`** (`overflow-wrap: break-word`) so a long unbroken string (URL / "aaaa…") wraps inside the card and never widens it past its container; collapsed titles get **`truncate`** (single line). Multi-line text uses `whitespace-pre-line break-words`. **Textarea inputs must wrap too (sprint 446):** every description/note/message `<textarea>` carries **`break-words`** so a long unbroken token wraps DOWNWARD inside the field and never overflows horizontally / widens the card (textareas only wrap at spaces by default). **Char limits (silent until reached):** note/description inputs carry a `maxLength` + a brief amber "Has alcanzado el límite de {max} caracteres." line shown ONLY at the cap (NO upfront counter): publicar-proyecto descripción **500**, booking note **300**, propuesta mensaje **500**, report details / cancel reason / cancel note **300** (`charLimit` key per namespace). The full note shows in the EXPANDED card (no nested "ver más" — the limit keeps it bounded).

> **UNIFIED card design language across ALL FOUR sections (sprint 440).** Solicitudes recibidas, Oportunidades, **Mis solicitudes**, and Mis publicaciones now share ONE cohesive collapsible-card system — **Mis solicitudes was converted from a flat card into the same accordion** so all four look like the same app. **Collapsed header (identical structure):** `<button w-full text-left p-4 flex items-center gap-2.5 hover:bg-[#fafafa]>` → optional **avatar `h-10`** (the 3 person-sections; Mis publicaciones has none — it's the client's own posting) → a `flex-1` block with a **`justify-between` top row = PRIMARY (name/título) + a status/categoría chip on the RIGHT**, then a **KEY-FACT line** below → a **`ChevronDown h-5 text-[#9ca3af]`** that rotates 180° when open. **TYPOGRAPHY HIERARCHY (fixes "everything is bold"):** the PRIMARY name/title is **`text-[15px] font-semibold text-[#111827]`** (semibold, the one prominent element — NOT bold); the KEY-FACT line is **`text-[13px]` with a muted `#6b7280` label + a `font-medium text-[#374151]` value** (readable, NOT bold); status chip = `Badge text-[11px] font-semibold`. So exactly ONE element stands out per card. Per-section PRIMARY · KEY-FACT: Solicitudes recibidas = client name · `Fecha:`; Oportunidades = título · `Presupuesto:`; Mis solicitudes = pro name · `Fecha:`; Mis publicaciones = título · `Propuestas:` (shown with a **`Users` people icon + N propuestas** — sprint 503, reads as "N personas interesadas"; dark `#374151`, was `MessageSquare`). Important flags (**Menor de edad**, **Adulto mayor**, **Sin verificar**, flagged) sit inline after the name only where they are decision-useful; for clinical age flags that means the pro's **Solicitudes recibidas**, not the client's **Mis solicitudes**. **Mis solicitudes expanded details do NOT repeat the professional identity/profile block** because the collapsed header already owns that information and "Ver perfil" belongs in the actions menu. Keep this expanded area mostly **flat**: service and beneficiary context render as label/value rows, not nested grey/blue boxes; reserve a boxed/tinted treatment only for actual state messages such as a professional cancellation reason. Beneficiary age displays in years, or months when under one year (`Edad: 6 meses`). **Mis publicaciones expanded details follow the same de-boxed rule:** the collapsed header owns title/status/count/date/zone; the expanded area shows the full description, actions, and proposals only. Proposals sort accepted -> pending -> declined; completed posts show the accepted proposal first and tuck unchosen proposals behind the existing disclosure. Do not add client-side post editing while proposals may be active unless a realtime/proposal-repricing model exists; use cancel/reopen/delete and a corrected new post instead. **Status colour is ONE shared mapping** (`STATUS_VARIANT`, identical client+pro, sprint 440): active/upcoming = `default` (brand-blue), awaiting confirmation = `warning` (amber), finished = `success` (green), cancelled = `error` (red), reprogramada = `muted` — so a booking's open-vs-closed state reads the same everywhere (previously the client styled active states green "success"/done and the finished state muted grey — inverted). Redundant statuses stay hidden per the filter (`solicitudStatusRedundant`/`proyectoStatusRedundant`). Below is the original accordion spec it builds on. **Card hover + rounded corners (sprint 441):** the cards are `rounded-2xl` but **NOT `overflow-hidden`** (overflow-hidden would clip the in-card actions menu) — so the collapsed `<button>` itself carries the rounded corners (`cn("…hover:bg-[#fafafa]", expanded ? "rounded-t-2xl" : "rounded-2xl")`) so its hover background never squares off the corners. **Actions = ONE primary + "···" overflow menu (sprint 441):** keep ONE most-used action visible (1 tap, never buried) + the secondary actions in the shared **`CardActionsMenu`** (`components/dashboard/card-actions-menu.tsx` — opens a small dropdown UPWARD, outside-click closes, destructive items red `text-red-600 hover:bg-red-50`). **The trigger is RESPONSIVE (sprint 442):** a compact **`···` icon on MOBILE** (`MoreHorizontal sm:hidden`), a **labelled "Acciones" + `ChevronDown`** (rotates when open) **on DESKTOP** (`hidden sm:inline` / `sm:block`). **Profile navigation goes in this menu, NOT a separate link (sprint 442):** because the whole collapsed header is the expand `<button>`, the name can't be a nested link — so "Ver perfil" (Mis solicitudes) is the FIRST, non-destructive menu item (`router.push('/profesionales/{slug}')`); don't re-add a standalone "Ver perfil" link. Footer = `flex items-center gap-2` with the primary `flex-1 sm:flex-none` and the menu pushed right (or `ml-auto` when there is no primary). Per section: **Solicitudes recibidas** primary = WhatsApp · menu = Marcar completado · Cancelar · Reportar; **Mis solicitudes** primary = Contactar / Confirmar finalización / Dejar reseña (by state) · menu = Reprogramar · Cancelar · Reportar; **Mis publicaciones** primary = Confirmar finalización / Dejar reseña / Reabrir (none for an open publicación) · menu = Cancelar publicación · Eliminar; **Oportunidades** primary = the «Enviar propuesta» form (no menu). Don't go back to a row of many buttons.

> **Sprint 269 override for Mis solicitudes actions.** In client Mis solicitudes, the professional name is allowed to be a link because the collapsed header is now a keyboard-accessible `div role="button"` instead of a nested-link button. This supersedes the older "Ver perfil in the actions menu" guidance for this section only: use primary state action + direct **Cancelar** for active bookings + compact flag icon for **Reportar**; offer Reprogramar inside the cancel confirmation.

> **Sprint 270 override for Solicitudes recibidas actions.** In professional Solicitudes recibidas, do NOT hide the core workflow behind an actions menu. The pro needs the next steps visible: WhatsApp first when a phone exists, then **Marcar completado** and red-outline **Cancelar** for active bookings, with **Reportar cliente** as the compact flag icon on the right. This supersedes the older "WhatsApp + Más overflow" guidance for this section; use wrapping direct buttons so phone layouts stay readable.

> **Sprint 271 override for dashboard WhatsApp + received-request details.** Dashboard WhatsApp buttons should read **Contactar** (or a similarly short action), not "WhatsApp a {name}", because the green brand button + glyph already communicate the channel and short labels fit phone layouts. Keep the WhatsApp glyph `shrink-0`. In Solicitudes recibidas expanded details, keep patient, phone/cedula, and note inside ONE compact white sheet with internal dividers; do not split "La cita es para" into a separate blue callout unless it is an actual warning/state message.

> **Report flag icon treatment.** Report actions use a simple red `Flag` icon, not a bordered/circular icon button. In cards, render it as a quiet `h-9 w-9 rounded-lg text-[#dc2626] hover:bg-[#fef2f2] hover:text-[#b91c1c]` icon action. In report modals/menus, keep the same plain red flag next to the label/title. Do not wrap report flags in permanent circles, rings, or grey bordered buttons.

> **Pro/client list cards are EXPANDABLE accordions (sprint 430).** Solicitudes recibidas, Oportunidades, and Mis publicaciones use a **collapsed-by-default, tap-to-expand** card (decluttered, many items visible at once): the WHOLE collapsed header is a `<button>` (`w-full text-left ... hover:bg-[#fafafa]`) showing ONLY the essentials — **who/título + the ONE key fact (appointment date / presupuesto / nº de propuestas) + status + Sin-verificar pill + a `ChevronDown` that rotates 180° when open**. Tapping reveals a bordered section (`border-t border-[#f3f4f6] pt-3`) with the full details (identity, callouts, zona·plazo, description, fecha) AND the actions/forms — so **buttons + extra text only appear on demand, never crammed by default**. One-at-a-time accordion (`expandedId`/`expandedProject` single id). Keep the zero-icon text-label style ("Fecha:", "Presupuesto:", "Tel:", …) in BOTH states. **Affordance (sprint 431):** the WHOLE collapsed card is the tappable `<button>` (tap anywhere — not just the chevron) and the chevron is a clear **`h-5 w-5`** `ChevronDown` rotating 180° when open; NO "Ver"/"Ver más" text. **Expanded ACTION layout (sprint 431, consistent across all three):** primary action(s) **full-width on mobile** (`w-full sm:w-auto`), the two outline secondaries (when present) share a **2-col row** (`grid grid-cols-2 gap-2 sm:contents`), everything **collapses to ONE tidy wrapping row on desktop**, and any **destructive/quiet minor action is a small text link** below (not a button: `self-start text-xs font-semibold text-[#9ca3af] hover:text-red-500`). Per section: **Solicitudes recibidas** → WhatsApp (green) full-width primary on mobile, then **"Marcar completado" (left) + "Cancelar" (right) ALWAYS share ONE row on mobile** (`grid grid-cols-2 gap-2 sm:contents` → 2-col on mobile, dissolves into the single wrapping row on desktop); the pro has no Reprogramar (sprint 433); label shortened to "Marcar completado" + `px-3` so both fit at ~360px (Button is `whitespace-nowrap`); Reportar text-link below (no "Más" overflow); **Mis publicaciones** → the status's primary (Confirmar finalización / Cancelar / Reabrir / Reseña) full-width, Eliminar text-link, then the propuestas list (accept/decline); **Oportunidades** → the Proponer form with a full-width "Enviar propuesta" primary. Touch targets ≥40px (header row); compact `size="sm"` buttons but full-width on mobile. Consistent across the three.

> **"Lead card" layout for Solicitudes recibidas + Oportunidades (sprint 421, COMPACTED sprint 426).** Both use the same modern card: `rounded-2xl` chrome → a **person/title header** (avatar + bold name/title + a chip on the right: status for solicitudes, categoría for oportunidades) → a **tinted "highlight" block** (`rounded-xl border border-[#eef0f2] bg-[#f9fafb]`, an uppercase eyebrow + a bold value) holding the ONE key fact = the scan anchor (**the appointment date** for solicitudes, **the presupuesto** for oportunidades, with secondary `servicio·zona` / `zona·plazo` beneath) → secondary text (Nota/description) → an actions footer. **Request-info block (Solicitudes recibidas, sprint 443) — organised, NOT a flat "label: value" dump:** (1) the patient callout (para-otra); (2) a **DETAILS card** (`rounded-xl border border-[#eef0f2] bg-[#f9fafb] overflow-hidden`) with the **SERVICE as a navy `#162543` semibold headline** + location muted, a `border-t` divider, then **CONTACT in a 2-col grid** with tiny uppercase eyebrow labels (`text-[10px] uppercase tracking-[0.06em] text-[#9ca3af]`) over `font-medium #374151` values; (3) a muted "Solicitada el {fecha}" meta; (4) the **NOTE as a quote** (`border-l-[3px] border-[#009FD9]/40 bg-[#f9fafb]` + a "Nota del cliente" eyebrow). Phone via `formatPhoneCR` ("+506 8888 8888"), date `{day,month-short,year}`. Use eyebrow-label + grouped cards (not inline "label: value") when a card has several supporting fields. Para-otra-persona shows a brand-tint "La cita es para" patient callout — the patient's **name + AGE IN YEARS** (`Edad: {n} años`, computed from `beneficiary_dob` via `computeAge`, leads instead of the raw DOB) and a **"Menor de edad" amber badge ONLY when under 18** (`isMinorFromDob`, or the `beneficiary_is_minor` flag for legacy rows without a DOB); **adults show just the age, no badge** (no "Mayor de edad") — sprint 436. Reuse this lead-card pattern for any "incoming item the pro acts on" list. **Compact/dense spec (sprint 426) — UberEats/CRED-style dense list cards so MORE fit per screen:** card body `px-4 pt-3.5 pb-3` (was `px-[18px] pt-4 pb-3.5`) + `gap-2.5` (was `gap-3.5`); avatar `h-10 w-10` (was `h-11`); highlight/callout blocks `px-3 py-2.5` / `px-3 py-2` (was `px-3.5 py-3` / `py-2.5`); the anchor value is `text-[15px]` (was `16px`); list gap `gap-3` (was `gap-4`); oportunidad description `line-clamp-2` (was 3). **Action buttons are COMPACT (Solicitudes recibidas):** the ONE primary **WhatsApp** is a small button (`size="sm"` pill); ALL secondaries (**Marcar completado · Reprogramar · Cancelar · Reportar**) live in a tidy **"Más" overflow menu** (a small bordered `h-8` pill `bookingRequests.moreActions` + `ChevronDown`, opens a right-aligned `shadow-xl` popover; outside-click closes via a `[data-card-menu]` guard + parent `menuFor` state) instead of a row of large full-width buttons dominating the card. Touch targets stay ≥32px. The cancel/reschedule inline panels are unchanged. **Don't return to the old big full-width 4-button action row** — keep the primary-+-overflow pattern for the pro's lead cards.

> **Zero-icon request sections (sprint 413).** The 4 request sections use **TEXT LABELS, not icons**, for every info field — the canonical label scheme: **Fecha: · Servicio: · Zona: · Nota: · Tel: · Cédula: · Nacimiento: · Presupuesto: · Plazo: · Publicado:** (muted `#9ca3af` label + value). NO calendar/location/money/phone/id/file/status icons; NO decorative leading icons on buttons. The ONLY icons that remain in these sections: the **WhatsApp brand mark** on WhatsApp buttons, the **expand/collapse chevrons** (functional), and the **large empty-state illustrations**. Apply this minimal label-not-icon treatment to any similar list/card section.

> **Field label/value contrast (sprint 554).** Any compact metadata row that uses an icon + field label + value must split contrast clearly: icon and label in muted grey (`#9ca3af`), actual value in readable dark text (`#374151`/`#111827`). Example: `Zona:` stays grey, `San José, San José` is dark. Do this consistently for Fecha, Servicio, Zona, Propuestas, Publicado, Edad, and similar fields. Real money/budget values may stay brand-blue (`#0089bb`) because they are decision anchors, but the label still stays grey. This matches the Cédula/Teléfono expanded-row pattern.

---

## 2. Design tokens

These are the **canonical** values. Use them via the existing primitives (`Button`, `Input`, `Badge`, `Card`, `Select`) wherever possible instead of re-deriving styles. Tailwind arbitrary values (`bg-[#009FD9]`) are how the codebase expresses these today — match the surrounding code.

### Color palette

**Brand (blue) — primary identity & primary actions**
| Token | Hex | Use |
|---|---|---|
| Brand | `#009FD9` | Primary buttons, links, focus ring, active states, verified badge |
| Brand hover | `#0089bb` | Primary button hover |
| Brand tint | `#EBF5FB` | Light brand background (chips, hovers, highlights) |
| Brand tint border | `#bfdbfe` | Border on brand-tint chips/badges |

**Accent (orange) — sparingly, for featured/secondary emphasis only.** Not the default CTA color.
| Token | Hex |
|---|---|
| Accent | `#ff7c0a` · hover `#f05f00` · text-on-tint `#c74600` · tint `#fff8ed` / border `#ffdba5` |

**WhatsApp** (the contact channel): `#25d366` · hover `#1da851`. Use only for the WhatsApp action.

**Neutrals (gray scale)**
| Hex | Use |
|---|---|
| `#111827` | Primary text / headings (foreground) |
